citeturn1058search4 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 2 weeks ago; @alexisgraycephotography

# Hello, I am Alexis!

My passion for photography was driven from moving a lot as a military family and I wanted a way to capture memories that I could take with me wherever God would take us next. I still find myself often looking at these memories with laughter. I am very sentimental and this is what makes me so passionate about photography. I believe that everyone should have moments to look back on throughout life!

I am a Photographer currently based right outside of Knoxville, TN but will be relocating to Detroit Michigan in August of 2026. I graduated Pellissippi Community College with my Associate's Degree in December 2020.

I offer a variety of different types of sessions including Senior/Graduation, Prom/Formal, Couple, Engagement, Wedding/Elopement, Family, Maternity, etc.

I love to meet new people and would LOVE to work with you!

### Follow me on Instagram

@alexisgraycephotography--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Les réalisateurs de Michael Jackson | Premiere.fr (https://www.premiere.fr/Cinema/Les-realisateurs-de-Michael-Jackson)
citeturn1058search5 [wordlim: 200] Published: 8.1 years ago; Crawled: 3 weeks ago; Aujourd’hui, il fait doucement ricaner, et reste surtout symptomatique de la perte de créativité immédiate de Bambi après l’ouragan Thriller. ... David Fincher ... Tourné pendant la période la plus bling-bling de la clipographie de MJ (des effets spéciaux, des guest stars, du cool et des pas de danse, partout, tout le temps), Who Is It pète à la tronche par son romantisme cradingue qui cite Hitchcock, Saul Bass et (donc) Vertigo à tout bout de plans, et son absence de frime revendiquée comme un acte de foi.Mieux, shooté par un Fincher essoré par le bide (et le remontage) de son Alien 3 et revanchard comme jamais, Who Is It peut s’envisager, sans trop d’exagération, comme le prototype de toute la filmo à venir de son auteur: on y décèle pêle-mêle des motifs qu’on retrouvera dans Seven (la traque, l’acteur qui ressemble à Morgan Freeman, la photo ocre), The Game (les cartes de visite) et Panic Room (l’expressionnisme architectural).
Et c’est comment ? Looké comme le méchant d’un sous-Mad Max italien, Michael s’amuse à frauder dans le métro avec des copains breakdancers, sous l’oeil à moitié assoupi d’un Scorsese qui se contente lui de faire tournoyer sa caméra dans tous les sens. Etrangement, le clip marque son époque. Aujourd’hui, il fait doucement ricaner, et reste surtout symptomatique de la perte de créativité immédiate de Bambi après l’ouragan Thriller.

iframe

 

David Fincher
Quand et pourquoi ? Ete 92, Who Is It est le cinquième single issu du LP Dangerous. David Fincher est embauché pour tourner le clip.
Et c’est comment ? : Mieux que tout. Tourné pendant la période la plus bling-bling de la clipographie de MJ (des effets spéciaux, des guest stars, du cool et des pas de danse, partout, tout le temps), Who Is It pète à la tronche par son romantisme cradingue qui cite Hitchcock, Saul Bass et (donc) Vertigo à tout bout de plans, et son absence de frime revendiquée comme un acte de foi. Mieux, shooté par un Fincher essoré par le bide (et le remontage) de son Alien 3 et revanchard comme jamais, Who Is It peut s’envisager, sans trop d’exagération, comme le prototype de toute la filmo à venir de son auteur: on y décèle pêle-mêle des motifs qu’on retrouvera dans Seven (la traque, l’acteur qui ressemble à Morgan Freeman, la photo ocre), The Game (les cartes de visite) et Panic Room (l’expressionnisme architectural). En filigrane le réal de Zodiac balance sa vision, troublante, du mythe Jackson, et décide, in fine, de l’intégrer à un mausolée toc-pop. Déjà mort, MJ?

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

citeturn1058search6 [wordlim: 200] Published: 16.0 years ago; Crawled: last week; David Royston Bailey est né le 2 janvier 1938 à Leytonstone, dans une banlieue de l’East end de Londres, à quelques pâtés de maison du lieu de naissance d’Alfred Hitchcock. ... A 13 ans, il remporte un prix, pour un dessin qu’il fait de Bambi, le petit faon, héro du dessin animé de Walt Disney.

# David Bailey

Par Administrator / 1 octobre 2010

David Royston Bailey est né le 2 janvier 1938 à Leytonstone, dans une banlieue de l’East end de Londres, à quelques pâtés de maison du lieu de naissance d’Alfred Hitchcock.

Sa famille déménage après le bombardement de leur maison, lors d’un raid aérien, pour s’installer à Heighman road dans le quartier d’East Ham, il à alors  trois ans. C’est là, que Thelma, sa petite sœur, et lui sont élevés par Gladys leur mère. Sa tante Dolly et son bull terrier, habite l’étage au dessus de la maison. Son père Bert qui exerce la profession de tailleur, ne tarde pas à quitter sa famille.


Image: La biographie de David Bailey

David s’intéresse à l’ornithologie.
A 10 ans, il rêve de devenir peintre.
Il prend sa première photographie de petits moineaux avec le Brownie, de sa mère, sans que ce soit une réussite.

Il souffre de dyslexie, ce qui explique sont manque de réussite à l’école.

A 13 ans, il remporte un prix, pour un dessin qu’il fait de Bambi, le petit faon, héro du dessin animé de Walt Disney.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Driven by a desire for all neighborhoods to offer residents a high quality of life, Alexis Butler is dedicated to creating systemic change in sustainable housing and community development.

Occupation: Senior Program Manager, Corporation for Supportive Housing
City: Kansas City, Missouri

  * By Rustandy Center
  * September 20, 2022
  * Civic Scholars

Why Chicago Booth?

I was looking for an environment that valued community collaboration, encouraged intellectual curiosity, and embedded theory and analytics in its coursework. Chicago Booth demonstrated a commitment to these areas and provided a culture that would challenge me to assess social phenomena and the world in new ways.

What skills are you looking to develop at Booth?   

My desire to work, and remain, in the social impact space is driven by my desire for all neighborhoods to be resource-rich environments that provide a high quality of life to all residents—a reality that remains far too distant for many marginalized communities. --------------------------------------------------------------------------------

citeturn1058search8 [wordlim: 200] Published: 4.5 years ago; Crawled: 3 weeks ago; Elliott leaves to cherish his memory: wife Christine of Upper Molboro, MD; daughter Quinisha (Denard) Cunningham of Hyattsville, MD; sons Kendall and Lydell Nickens of South Carolina; grandchildren Destiny and Judah Cunningham; loving mother Irene King of Enterprise, MS; sisters Mary King and Rosie Carothers of Racine, WI; Vivian and Wendy King of Enterprise, MS; brothers Ben and Carliss King of Racine, WI; Armond (Danita) King of Alpharetta, "'"'GA and Tracy King of Killeen, TX; aunts Willie Mae Chambliss of Meridian, MS and Jean King of Racine, WI; Great Aunt Ruth Sanders of Meridian, MS and a host of other relatives and friends. ... Photo/Video
He retired from the air force after 20 years of service, in 1989. Elliott worked various jobs after his retirement. His last place of employment was the Government Prentice Office where he worked as a chef until 2008. He returned to Enterprise, MS in 2009 to be closer to his family. Due to his declining health in 2021 he relocated to Hyattsville, MO to live with his daughter. Elliott had a great sense of humor and was very entertaining. He enjoyed cooking, fishing, traveling and visiting casinos trying his luck. But most of all he enjoyed being with his family, especially playing with his grandchildren. Elliott leaves to cherish his memory: wife Christine of Upper Molboro, MD; daughter Quinisha (Denard) Cunningham of Hyattsville, MD; sons Kendall and Lydell Nickens of South Carolina; grandchildren Destiny and Judah Cunningham; loving mother Irene King of Enterprise, MS; sisters Mary King and Rosie Carothers of Racine, WI; Vivian and Wendy King of Enterprise, MS; brothers Ben and Carliss King of Racine, WI; Armond (Danita) King of Alpharetta, "'"'GA and Tracy King of Killeen, TX; aunts Willie Mae Chambliss of Meridian, MS and Jean King of Racine, WI; Great Aunt Ruth Sanders of Meridian, MS and a host of other relatives and friends. He was preceded in death by his father Spencer King Sr; sisters Minnie and Dorothy King; brothers Mark King and Spencer King Jr.; grandparents Henry Cotton, Rosetta and William Chambliss, Bennie King Sr. and Alma King.

citeturn1058news19 [wordlim: 100] Published: last year; He made his European film debut in Goodbye Again in 1961, which earned him a Best Actor Bravo Otto nomination, a Bambi Award nomination, the Cannes Film Festival Award for Best Actor, and a David di Donatello Award for Best Actor. ... Tom Hollander as Alfred Hitchcock in episode 302 of Monster: The Ed Gein Story.
Newman, a well-known proponent of conversion therapy, suggests he take care of this affliction with a lobotomy, electroshock, or conversion therapy.
Of course, we'll never know the exact conversations that Perkins and Newman had, but it is reported that Perkins agreed to undergo conversion therapy. Soon after, he married Berenson.

Perkins felt typecast after acting in 'Psycho.'

The role of Norman Bates was a career-defining one for Perkins-for better or worse. Perkins allegedly felt that he was typecast as a villain in Hollywood. He even bought out the remainder of his Paramount contract to move to Europe and start his career afresh there. He made his European film debut in Goodbye Again in 1961, which earned him a Best Actor Bravo Otto nomination, a Bambi Award nomination, the Cannes Film Festival Award for Best Actor, and a David di Donatello Award for Best Actor.
